# Five-angle review of every PinRef build — 2026-11-09

Companion to PINOUT-AUDIT.md (Pass 3). Five independent subagents per connector cluster — Documentation, Power and damage, Circuit, Field practice, Red team — each saw only the build data, the connector pinouts and the list of sources opened in Chrome. Every disagreement they raised is listed here, build by build, with the fix applied (or the reason none was) and the source behind each fix. Round 1 reviewed all 83 builds (55 agents). Round 2 re-reviewed every build that changed or drew a disagreement with fresh agents. Round 3 was a final independent pass over every build that carries power. Round 4 re-reviewed, with all five angles, every build that drew a disagreement in round 2 or 3 (55 builds); its section, at the end, also says what was not applied and why.

## Round 1 (all 83 builds)



Angles: D = documentation, P = power & damage, C = circuit, F = field practice, R = red team. Verdict agree/disagree with the worst severity each angle assigned. Every disagreeing issue is listed in one line (full text with evidence in the scratch aggregate, kept with the session).

**#1 XLR3 Mic Cable (Straight-Through)** — C:ok D:minor F:ok P:minor R:ok
- [C] The 'use' text gives one party-line pin map for both brands ('pin 2 +30 VDC + channel 1 audio, pin 3 channel 2 audio'). That is the RTS map only; on Clear-Com pin 2 is power with no audio and pin 3 is the single audio line. Wiring is unaffected (1-1, 2-2, 3-3 …
- [D] The 'use' text attributes the RTS two-channel line pinout to 'Clear-Com / RTS' — 'pin 2 +30 VDC + channel 1 audio, pin 3 channel 2 audio'. On a Clear-Com party line pin 2 is DC only and the single audio channel is on pin 3; there is no audio on pin 2 and no se…
- [F] The 'use' text describes the party line as 'pin 2 +30 VDC + channel 1 audio, pin 3 channel 2 audio' for both Clear-Com and RTS. That is the RTS 2-channel layout only. Clear-Com's single-channel line carries audio on pin 3 and DC only on pin 2, and Telex Audioc…
- [P] The 'use' text advertises this cable for the Clear-Com/RTS party line (+30 VDC on pin 2) but the notes give no caution about what that voltage does when the same cable lands on ordinary audio gear. Realistic paths: (a) a party-line cable plugged into a dynamic…
- [R] The party-line description in 'use' is RTS-only and wrong for Clear-Com: it says pin 2 carries '+30 VDC + channel 1 audio' and pin 3 'channel 2 audio', but on a Clear-Com single-channel line pin 2 is DC only and pin 3 is the only audio.
- **Fix:** use text gave the RTS two-channel pin map for both brands; Clear-Com has DC only on pin 2 and its single audio channel on pin 3 — *source:* Clear-Com SF 158784598; RTS BP-325 manual p.13
- **Fix:** added 30 V party-line caution — *source:* Clear-Com SF 158784598; RS-701 QRG

**#2 XLR3 to XLR5 — Pass-Through** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:minor R:ok
- [F] Informational only: this is pin-for-pin identical to the stock DMX 3-to-5 turnaround sold by Neutrik (NA3F5M/NA5F3M), Hosa (DMX306), Chauvet, Titan AV etc., so it will inter-work with any of those at the far end. The note correctly warns that it is not a heads…
- [P] The notes explicitly endorse sending the 30 VDC party line down the 5-pin run ('a straight wire passes it fine') but do not warn that the run is physically a 5-pin HEADSET extension. Any 5-pin headset (RTS PH-88R5, male XLR5) plugged into that run — the obviou…
- [P] The XLR5 gender is not stated, so the damage analysis depends on which way the adapter is built. With a MALE XLR5 it mates a beltpack/panel headset jack (BP-325 XLR-5-31 and Clear-Com XLR-5F are female): the pack's mic DC bias on pin 2 and its mic preamp are t…
- [R] Notes say the party line's 30 VDC 'passes fine' but do not warn that the energised 5-pin end now looks exactly like a headset jack or DMX port and will destroy a headset mic or a DMX receiver if plugged in.
- [R] Genders of the two cables are not stated; a pair built with the same genders will not mate with the 5-pin run.
- **Fix:** added gender statement and energised-XLR5 mis-plug caution — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13 (XLR-5-31 pinout); Clear-Com SF 158784598; DMX512 Wikipedia

**#3 XLR3 to XLR5 — Mic into Intercom Beltpack (Mic Input)** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:minor R:ok
- [F] Informational: matches what Clear-Com itself tells users to do with a 3-pin XLR mic on a beltpack headset jack (unbalanced: hot to pin 2, ground to pin 1, dynamic mic only), and the pin-1/pin-3 tie is the same scheme the NIDA comms tester uses on the mic side.…
- [P] Voltages on this cable in normal use are benign: pack mic bias (2.5 V via 1 kΩ on Clear-Com FSII; ~5 V on RTS KP-32 panels) flows through the dynamic capsule from XLR5 pin 2 to pin 1 at ~2 mA, and RS-701-style 15 V headset DC is on pin 4, which is dead-ended. …
- [R] The XLR5 end of this cable is male, but the connector is drawn as a female face (faceGender 'female', pin 1 upper-right, pin 2 right, pin 3 bottom). The male insert the tech actually solders is the mirror image, so a tech working from position rather than the …
- **Fix:** added male-insert mirror warning — *source:* connector data: xlr5.faceGender = female

**#4 XLR3 to XLR5 — Balanced Feed to Both Ears of a Headset** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:ok R:ok
- [F] Informational: no manufacturer sells this exact cable; it is a custom A2 build. Pin use is consistent with every 5-pin headset wiring found (pin 3 common return, pins 4/5 L/R hots, including David Clark camera headsets which put all ear lows on pin 3). PinRef'…
- **Fix:** none needed (all angles agreed) 

**#5 XLR3 to 1/4" TRS — Balanced Audio** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:minor R:ok
- [P] No caution about phantom power. With the XLR3 end in a mic input that has +48 V on, 48 V (via 6.81 kΩ) sits on TRS Tip (from XLR pin 2) and Ring (from XLR pin 3) relative to Sleeve; if the TRS end is on a line or headphone OUTPUT, that DC is pushed into the ou…
- [P] No caution about intercom party-line XLR3s. An XLR3 end plugged into a Clear-Com or RTS line jack puts +30 VDC (pin 2) on TRS Tip and, on RTS, channel-2 audio (pin 3) on Ring; that DC goes straight into whatever the TRS end is on.
- [P] No caution that plugging the TRS end into a mono TS jack grounds Ring, which grounds XLR pin 3 — the cold leg of an active balanced output.
- **Fix:** added phantom, party-line and TS-jack cautions — *source:* Yamaha phantom; Clear-Com SF 158784598; Rane 151

**#6 XLR3 to RJ45 — Audio over Cat5** — C:minor D:major F:major P:minor R:dangerous
- [C] The 'use' line offers this as a Clear-Com party-line cable, but Clear-Com's 3-pin line carries DC on pin 2 and unbalanced audio on pin 3 referenced to pin 1. Through this build that puts the 30 V party-line supply on RJ45 pin 1 (StudioHub's L+ audio input) and…
- [C] Channel 2's ground (XLR pin 1) lands on RJ45 pin 5, which is n/c on every StudioHub+ panel. If one end of the run is a real StudioHub+ port, ch2's XLR pin 1 is open: no shield/ground reference and no phantom-power return for that channel. The note covers 'both…
- [D] The 'use' text names Clear-Com as a job for this cable, but the cited source (StudioHub+) says nothing about Clear-Com, and Clear-Com's own published Cat5 pinout is different. On a Clear-Com party line XLR3 pin 2 carries the 30 V DC supply, so this layout puts…
- [F] The 'use' line says 'Clear-Com', but the only Clear-Com-published XLR3-to-RJ45 adapter pinout is incompatible with this build. If a PinRef XLR3→RJ45 is used at one end of a Cat5 run and a Clear-Com-pinout adapter at the other, XLR3 pin 2 (+30 V on an analog pa…
- [F] Putting XLR3 pin 1 on RJ45 pin 4 (ch1) and pin 5 (ch2) is a PinRef-only convention. No manufacturer adapter does this: StudioHub/Axia/Telos put pin 1 on the shield only; Radial, Whirlwind, ETS and RAT boxes use pins 4/5 as another channel's hot/cold. A PinRef …
- [F] Signal pins (XLR pin 2 → RJ45 1, pin 3 → RJ45 2; ch2 on 3/6) match the StudioHub/Axia/Telos convention and also Whirlwind Catdusa CH1/CH2, but not Radial Catapult (orange pair = CH4) or RAT/ETS (orange pair = ch2). Worth naming the conventions in the notes.
- [P] The stated use is 'Clear-Com or audio balun', but the notes never say that on a party-line this cable puts +30 VDC onto RJ45 pin 1 (return on pin 4). The same RJ45 pinout is PinRef's audio pinout (builds 33/35), so a Cat5 run carrying Clear-Com/RTS that is pat…
- [P] Phantom path not mentioned. Because PinRef lands XLR pin 1 on RJ45 pin 4, 48 V phantom applied at a mixer input on the far end (build 33) flows RJ45 1/2 → XLR3 pins 2/3 of this build and returns via pin 4 → pin 1. That is fine for a mic, but if this XLR is on …
- [R] Worst realistic failure: the 'use' text says 'Clear-Com or audio balun'. A tech runs a Clear-Com/RTS party-line over this cable. XLR3 pin 2 on a party line is the DC rail (Clear-Com: 'Pin 2 = Voltage'; RTS BP-325: '2 +30 VDC in & audio'), so RJ45 pin 1 (Wht/Or…
- [R] Second failure: Clear-Com's own RJ45 party-line layout (HelixNet) is pin-incompatible with this cable. A tech plugs this cable into a HelixNet RJ45 port because the use text says 'Clear-Com': HelixNet pin 4 = XLR pin 2 (line DC + audio) lands on this cable's R…
- [R] Interop failure with real StudioHub+ gear: XLR pin 1 is landed on RJ45 pin 4, which StudioHub+ defines as 'DC GND' — the return rail for the ±15 V DC-Link (fused at 1/2 A). Against an official ADAPT-XLR at the far end, pin 1 is on the cable shield and pin 4 is…
- [R] Diagram risk, minor: the XLR3 face is drawn as the female face (pin 2 left, pin 1 right, pin 3 bottom), which matches the StudioHub+ guide's female drawing, so the face view itself is correct. But a tech soldering a female from the rear while reading the face …
- **Fix:** removed the Clear-Com claim: party-line pin 2 carries 30 V DC and Clear-Com's own Cat5 layout (HelixNet) is pin-incompatible — *source:* Clear-Com HelixNet tip; Clear-Com SF 158784598; StudioHub+ guide
- **Fix:** added party-line, HelixNet, snake-convention and StudioHub interop cautions — *source:* Clear-Com HelixNet tip; Radial Catapult spec; CatPatch table; StudioHub+ guide

**#7 XLR3 to DB25 — Tascam/AES59 Audio Snake** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:ok R:minor
- [D] The orientation statement 'Channels run right-to-left across the connector — Ch1 is at the far end' is view-dependent and is the opposite of Tascam's own diagram. It is only true when looking at the male plug's mating face (pin 1 top-left); when soldering the …
- [R] Orientation statement will mislead a tech soldering the male plug: the app's face view puts pin 1 at top-left (topRow 1..13 left to right, faceGender male) and the use text says 'channels run right-to-left', but Tascam's own drawing is the opposite hand and it…
- **Fix:** orientation statement was view-dependent and opposite to Tascam's drawing — *source:* TASCAM DB-25 Pinout Standard PDF
- **Fix:** replaced view-dependent orientation claim — *source:* TASCAM DB-25 Pinout Standard PDF

**#8 XLR3 to XLR4 — Balanced Feed to Headset Earphone** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:ok R:ok
- [C] Wiring is correct (XLR3 pin 2 → XLR4 pin 4, XLR3 pin 1 → XLR4 pin 3, cold floating), but the note's reason for floating cold is wrong for this cable: it says the earphone-return node 'is chassis-grounded inside Clear-Com / RTS panels'. This cable feeds a heads…
- [F] The note's reason for floating XLR3 pin 3 ('that node is chassis-grounded inside Clear-Com / RTS panels') does not apply to this cable, which feeds a headset: the earpiece is a floating transducer, so XLR3 pin 3 tied to XLR4 pin 3 would not be a short to groun…
- [F] Missing caution: Telex Audiocom (BP-1002/BP-2002) 4-pin wiring is reversed on the earphone pair (pin 3 = headphone high, pin 4 = headphone low), opposite to Clear-Com/RTS. A headset wired to Telex Audiocom convention still works on this cable but with inverted…
- [R] Notes say the male XLR4 end mates the Clear-Com headset, implying it cannot reach a pack; it does fit any RTS 4-pin pack, whose headset jack is female.
- **Fix:** corrected the stated reason for floating cold (earphone return is a floating transducer node, not a chassis ground) — *source:* Rane Note 151 Fig. 5
- **Fix:** added Telex polarity note and RTS-pack mis-plug caution — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13 (XLR-4-31 receptacle)

**#9 XLR3 to XLR4 — Mic into Intercom Beltpack (Mic Input)** — C:ok D:minor F:ok P:minor R:ok
- [D] The note says 'The beltpack jack is a male XLR4 (Clear-Com), so this cable needs a FEMALE XLR4', but the use text covers 'a 4-pin Clear-Com / RTS beltpack or panel'. The RTS BP-325 MONO HEADSET jack is a female XLR-4 receptacle and RTS 4-pin headsets (PH-88R) …
- [F] Informational: identical to Clear-Com's own guidance for a 3-pin dynamic mic into a 4-pin beltpack (unbalanced, hot pin 2, ground pin 1), and to the mic side of the NIDA comms tester. Gender call (female XLR4 to the pack's male chassis jack) matches Clear-Com.…
- [P] Normal-use voltages are safe: the Clear-Com RS-701 puts 15 V DC (20 mA) on headset pin 4 with pin 1 as DC−; pin 4 is dead-ended here so the 15 V is isolated, and pin 1 (DC−) tied to XLR3 pins 1 and 3 is just ground. Pack mic bias (2.5 V via 1 kΩ) through the d…
- [R] Gender statement covers Clear-Com only ('beltpack jack is a male XLR4 ... FEMALE XLR4'); the 'use' also names RTS packs, whose 4-pin headset jack is female, so for RTS the cable needs a MALE XLR4 and a tech building to the note gets a cable that does not mate.
- **Fix:** gender note was Clear-Com-only; RTS BP-325 has a female headset receptacle — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13; Clear-Com SF 158808488

**#10 XLR3 to BNC — Not Compatible** — C:ok D:ok F:major P:ok R:minor
- [C] The 'use' text frames the mismatch as 110 Ω vs 75 Ω, which is the AES3 digital-audio case, not analog balanced audio (an analog mic/line XLR has no 110 Ω characteristic impedance requirement). For AES3 there IS a legitimate passive adaptation: a transformer ba…
- [D] The build's only cited source (SDI Wikipedia) does not mention XLR, 110 Ω or balanced audio, so it does not support the '110Ω vs 75Ω' claim in the use text.
- [F] The blanket 'cannot be adapted with a passive cable' is contradicted by everyday field practice: a passive XLR3-to-BNC cable is the standard sound-department / A2 LTC timecode jam cable (recorder or slate XLR TC out to camera BNC TC in, or BNC TC out to record…
- [F] AES3 (110Ω XLR) to AES-3id (75Ω BNC) is also a common, sold adapter — a passive impedance transformer, not a cable. The build says 'cannot be adapted' without mentioning that this is the standard answer for digital audio.
- [R] The 'use' text says XLR3 and BNC 'Cannot be adapted with a passive cable.' That is true for SDI but false in general: passive XLR3-to-BNC adapters are a stock Neutrik part used for LTC timecode, word clock and AES3-id. A tech who knows this will distrust the p…
- **Fix:** STATUS CHANGE not_possible → partial: added the LTC/unbalanced-audio wiring (pin 2 → centre, pin 1 → shell, pin 3 float/tie); kept SDI and AES3 as not adaptable — *source:* Sound Devices 970 User Guide p.60 (BNC timecode unbalanced); Rane Note 151

**#11 XLR3 to Triax — Not Compatible** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:ok R:ok
- [C] Correct to refuse. The 'use' text says triax carries DC power but gives no magnitude; a tech with an XLR mic cable in hand should be told the triax port can carry well over 100 V DC, so even probing is unsafe.
- [D] Missing safety caution: a live triax carries lethal-level DC on the centre conductor; the note should say so, since an XLR adapter would put that on XLR pins.
- **Fix:** added voltage magnitude — *source:* Sony HXCU-100 manual p.8

**#12 XLR3 to DT12 — Channel Breakout** — C:minor D:ok F:ok P:ok R:minor
- [C] deadEnds entry is wrong for the multi-channel build it describes: it marks DT12 pin 2 as a dead end and says 'pins 2–36 unused', but pin 2 is Ch2 Ground and pins 2–36 are all live once more than one XLR is added (the build allows 12).
- [C] Notes assert the pin layout 'follows the standard Whirlwind/Clark DT12 industry convention' with no caution, but the cited source itself warns that manufacturers deviate — a mis-pinned DT12 at the other end would put another channel's hot on this XLR's pin 1/s…
- [D] The deadEnds entry is wrong: it is placed on DT12 pin 2 and says 'DT12 pins 2–36 unused (other channels)'. Pin 2 is Ch2 Ground and pins 2–36 are all used by the other 11 channels in this very build; the only genuinely unused pin is 37.
- [D] The cited Whirlwind source does not contain any pinout, so it does not support the wiring or the note 'standard Whirlwind/Clark DT12 industry convention'. Only Clark's table was verifiable, and Clark itself warns the pinout is not universal.
- [D] dt12 connector facePositions do not represent the real 37-pin insert and the connector has no faceGender, so the face view cannot be a correct mirror of anything. The app arranges pins in 12 channel-triangles across four horizontal rows plus two strays (pin 22…
- [F] deadEnds anchors the 'other channels unused' note on DT12 pin 2, but pin 2 is Ch2 Ground and is wired as soon as a second channel is added; the dead-end text also says 'pins 2–36 unused' while the single-channel view already uses pins 5 and 6.
- [F] Notes should carry the field caution that FK-37 shells are not always DT12-wired: Canare sells a 16-channel snake on the same FK37 connector, and Clark warns manufacturers deviate.
- [R] The dead-end marker is on a live pin. deadEnds marks DT12 pin 2 as unused, but pin 2 is Ch2 Ground and is wired in channel 2 of the same build; the description 'pins 2–36 unused' is false (pins 2–36 are channels 2–12; only pin 37 is spare). In a multi-channel …
- [R] Notes claim an 'industry convention' without the vendor's own caveat. Clark, the cited source, warns that manufacturers may not follow the table; Whirlwind says the layout is standardized on the Cannon FK-37 but publishes no table on the page. A tech wiring a …
- **Fix:** deadEnd marker was on a live pin (pin 2 = Ch2 ground) and 'industry convention' lacked Clark's caveat — *source:* Clark Wire DT12 pinout page
- **Fix:** deadEnds: removed the wrong marker on pin 2; only pin 37 is spare — *source:* Clark Wire DT12 pinout page

**#13 XLR3 to 3.5mm TRS — Mono Feed to a Stereo Input (Both Channels)** — C:major D:ok F:minor P:major R:major
- [C] The use line names 'phone, laptop' as 3.5mm STEREO inputs. A phone (and a laptop combo) 3.5mm jack is a headphone OUTPUT on Tip and Ring with the only input on the mic contact; this cable drives XLR pin 2 (a +4 dBu line output) into the phone's two headphone-a…
- [C] Level note says 'expect ~12 dB mismatch'. Taking only pin 2 against pin 1 from a differential output delivers half the balanced voltage (−6 dB), so the mismatch into a −10 dBV input is nearer 6 dB, not 12, for cross-coupled/differential outputs. Minor arithmet…
- [C] If the 3.5mm input is a camera's MIC input (listed as a destination), it is mic level and often carries plug-in power DC on Tip/Ring, so a +4 dBu line feed is ~40–50 dB too hot and the DC lands on XLR pin 2. The note only mentions a 12 dB mismatch.
- [F] Level note undersells the mismatch for the "camera" use case: the commercial cables of this exact wiring class are sold as camcorder MICROPHONE cables, i.e. the 3.5 mm camera jack is normally a mic-level input, not a −10 dBV line input, so "~12 dB" is wrong fo…
- [P] The 'use' lists 'phone' and 'laptop' as targets, but a phone's 3.5 mm jack (and most laptop 3.5 mm combo jacks) is a headphone/headset OUTPUT, not a stereo input: Tip = Left out, Ring 1 = Right out, Ring 2 = ground, Sleeve = mic. This cable's Tip–Ring jumper (…
- [P] No caution that the female XLR3 end fits a beltpack's male line loop-through connector: Clear-Com/RTS pin 2 carries +30 VDC, which this build puts on BOTH Tip and Ring of the 3.5 mm plug into a camera, recorder or phone.
- [R] The headline targets 'phone, laptop' have no 3.5 mm line INPUT: their jack is a headset jack whose Tip and Ring are headphone-amp OUTPUTS and whose only input is the mic contact on the Sleeve. This cable jumpers Tip to Ring, so into a phone it (a) ties the pho…
- **Fix:** phones and most laptop 3.5 mm jacks are headphone OUTPUTS (CTIA), so the Tip–Ring jumper would tie two amplifier outputs together — *source:* AOSP headset spec; Rane Note 109
- **Fix:** corrected level figure, added camera-mic, headset-jack and party-line cautions — *source:* Rane Note 110 (6 dB single-ended loss); AOSP headset spec; Rane 109; Clear-Com SF 158784598

**#14 XLR3 to TS — Balanced to Unbalanced** — C:ok D:ok F:minor P:ok R:ok
- [C] Wiring is correct and safe for active outputs. Note wording: for a true transformer-floating output, tying pin 3 to pin 1 is not merely a 'DC reference' — with pin 3 floating the secondary has no return path and the cable passes almost no signal. Same for a dy…
- [F] The pin-3 guidance in the notes misclassifies cross-coupled (servo-balanced) outputs: PinRef says FLOAT pin 3 for "active/servo-balanced" outputs and tie only for transformers, but Rane's interconnect chart groups transformer AND cross-coupled output stages to…
- **Fix:** note mis-stated which outputs need pin 3 tied — *source:* Rane Note 151 Fig. 5; Rane Note 110

**#15 XLR3 to RCA — Pro to Consumer** — C:ok D:ok F:minor P:minor R:ok
- [C] Same transformer-output caveat as build 14: pin 3 floating gives no signal from a floating transformer/cross-coupled output; the note frames tying pin 3 as merely 'safer to float' without saying when it is required.
- [F] Same note problem as build 14: "Pin 3 should float for transformerless active outputs" is stated without the cross-coupled exception; Rane ties pin 3 to pin 1 for cross-coupled output stages as well as transformers, and floating them loses 6 dB.
- [P] No caution about the one realistic DC path: the female XLR3 end fits a beltpack's male intercom loop-through connector (e.g. to 'record the PL' into a consumer recorder/receiver). Pin 2 then puts +30 VDC on the RCA centre into a consumer input whose coupling c…
- **Fix:** same cross-coupled caveat; added party-line caution — *source:* Rane Note 151; Clear-Com SF 158784598

**#16 XLR5 Straight-Through** — C:ok D:minor F:ok P:ok R:ok
- [D] The notes label DMX pins 4 and 5 as 'spare / spare'. DMX512-A assigns them to the second data link (pin 4 Data 2 −, pin 5 Data 2 +). The cable is unaffected (all five wired straight), but the label is wrong.
- [F] Informational: a 1:1 five-conductor extension is a stock rental item (Point Source Audio CM-EXT-5, Sescom NA5MF barrel) and is agnostic to the headset wiring variant in use, so it inter-works with everything. Note that two incompatible XLR5 headset conventions…
- [R] The 'use' offers the same cable as a DMX512 extension without saying it must then be built on 120 ohm DMX-rated cable, not mic cable.
- **Fix:** DMX pins 4/5 are the second data link, not spare — *source:* DMX512 Wikipedia (XLR-5 pinout)
- **Fix:** added DMX cable note — *source:* DMX512 Wikipedia

**#17 XLR5 to XLR4 — Intercom Beltpack to Headset** — C:ok D:ok F:ok P:ok R:ok
- [F] Informational: pins 1-4 straight and pin 5 open is the Clear-Com-recommended adapter and the one sold commercially (Point Source Audio ADP-4Mx5M / ADP-5FX4F, Pliant FleXLR-45). However 'never bridge pin 5 to pin 4' is not universal field practice: Clear-Com's …
- [R] Both ends of this cable are male, but both connectors are drawn as female faces; a male insert viewed from its mating face is the mirror image, so the drawing shows pin 1 and pin 5 (and 2/4) on the wrong sides for what the tech is soldering.
- [R] Note states 'a Clear-Com headset has a female XLR4' as if universal; Telex/RTS PH-88R and Riedel headsets carry a MALE XLR4, for which this cable's XLR4 end must be female.
- **Fix:** added mirror and headset-gender caveat — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13; Clear-Com SF 158841161

**#18 XLR5 to RJ45 — Intercom over Cat5** — C:minor D:minor F:major P:minor R:dangerous
- [C] Circuit works but the pairing does not follow the StudioHub+ convention the build claims to extend, and it is the worst choice for crosstalk: mic hot (XLR5 pin 2 → RJ45 1) is twisted with the EARPHONE return (XLR5 pin 3 → RJ45 2), while the mic's own return (X…
- [C] PoE caution is incomplete. It names only pins 4/5 and 7/8 (Mode B). Mode A PSEs put the 44–57 V across pairs 1/2 and 3/6, which in this build are mic hot + ear return and the two earphone hots.
- [C] Notes require shielded Cat5 but no wiring entry or note says where the drain lands, even though the XLR5 connector defines a shell pseudo-pin ('T — Shell / Tab — Cable Shield Drain'). Left unspecified, builders will either float the shield (no benefit) or tie …
- [C] Which end this build is (beltpack vs headset) is set only by XLR gender, which the build never states; #18 and #34 are the same mapping so gender is the only thing that distinguishes them in practice.
- [D] The cited StudioHub+ guide does not support the 'mic-ground on Blue (RJ45 pin 4)' claim. StudioHub+ puts XLR pin 1 on the cable shield, not on RJ45 pin 4, and lists pin 4 as DC GND; the build's note presents the pin-4 choice as the StudioHub+ XLR3 convention.
- [D] Face gender is wrong for this end of the run. The source end plugs into the beltpack/panel chassis, which is XLR-5 female (RTS headsets ship with a 5-pin male plug; the V-Series panel connector is XLR-5F), so this cable's XLR5 must be a male plug. The connecto…
- [D] PoE caution gives the wrong reason. The note says PoE puts 48 V on pins 4/5 and 7/8 only; IEEE 802.3af/at Alternative A puts the same 44–57 V on pins 1/2 and 3/6, which are exactly the pins carrying mic signal, ear return and the ear outputs in this build.
- [D] Cross-reference to build #4 as the verified XLR3 precedent cannot be checked from this cluster; the mirror build (#34) and the XLR4 builds refer to this build as '#15', but its id is 18.
- [F] Pair assignment is the opposite of manufacturer and A1/A2 practice: mic hot (XLR5 pin 2 → RJ45 1) and earphone return (XLR5 pin 3 → RJ45 2) share one twisted pair, while mic ground (XLR5 pin 1 → RJ45 4) and the ear hots (XLR5 pins 4, 5 → RJ45 3, 6) are each sp…
- [F] Rationale misstates the StudioHub+ convention. StudioHub+ puts the + and − of ONE balanced signal on RJ45 1/2 and sends XLR pin 1 to the cable shield, not to RJ45 pin 4. This build puts two unrelated signals (mic hot, ear return) on RJ45 1/2 and routes XLR5 pi…
- [F] Missing caution for the one RJ45 intercom port a headset user is most likely to meet: Clear-Com HelixNet powerline RJ45 (HelixNet beltpacks have 4-pin/5-pin XLR headset jacks and RJ45 ports side by side). Plugging this cable's RJ45 into a HelixNet RJ45 puts +3…
- [F] Cross-reference in notes points at the wrong build id: 'verified for build #4' cannot be checked and the mirror is referred to elsewhere as #15, but this build's id is 18 and its mirror is 34.
- [P] The PoE warning names the wrong pins for this cable. It says '48 V on pins 4/5 and 7/8' (mode B), but in this build pins 5, 7 and 8 are unused, so mode-B power has no return path. The pins that actually carry the headset are 1/2 and 3/6, which are exactly 802.…

- [R] The PoE caution names the wrong pins. IEEE 802.3af Alternative A (the common endspan-switch mode) puts 48 V on pairs 1/2 and 3/6 — exactly the pins carrying mic hot, ear return and both earphones in this build; 4/5 & 7/8 is Alternative B / passive PoE. A tech …
- [R] Notes require shielded Cat5 but no wiring entry terminates the shield: the XLR5 shell/tab (defined in the connector as 'Cable Shield Drain') and the RJ45 plug shell are not in the wiring list, so a tech building exactly from the diagram leaves the drain floati…
- [R] Pairing fights the cable: mic hot (RJ45 1) is twisted with earphone RETURN (RJ45 2) while mic ground sits alone on RJ45 4, and both earphone hots (RJ45 3, 6) return via RJ45 2 on another pair. Earphone drive current (up to 8 V p-p) loops across pairs beside a …
- [R] Cross-reference error: notes say the far end is '#15' and that the XLR3 convention was 'verified for build #4', but the mating build in this data set is id 34 and this build is id 18. A tech following the text opens the wrong build for the far end.
- [R] No gender stated per end. RTS/HelixNet-X5 beltpacks have a 5-pin FEMALE jack and RTS headsets a 5-pin MALE plug, so the beltpack end of this extension must be male and the headset end female; both ends are drawn as a female face. A tired tech who builds the ma…
- **Fix:** WIRING CHANGE: mic pair 1/2, left ear 3/6, right ear 5/4 (return fans out to 6 and 4); shield to XLR shell; gender stated; notes rewritten; PoE pins corrected; HelixNet caution; cross-refs fixed — *source:* Clear-Com SF 158845384 / 158755357 (separate shielded pairs for 1/2 and 3/4, 15 ft, never bond mic and earphone grounds); Clear-Com HelixNet tip; PoE Wikipedia; RS-701 QRG; RTS BP-325 manual p.13

- [D] Missing caution: if this hack is plugged into a Tascam/AES59-format DB25 on a mic preamp or recorder with phantom power on, DB25 pin 1 (Ch8 Hot) and pin 4 (Ch6 Hot) could put +48 V onto XLR5 pin 1 (mic ground) and XLR5 pin 4 (left earphone), and pin 3 (Ch7 Col…

- [R] Mis-plug into Tascam analog gear is worse than 'miswired audio' if that port is a phantom-powered mic input. DB25 pins 1–5 on a Tascam device are Ch8 Hot, Ch8 Gnd, Ch7 Cold, Ch6 Hot, Ch6 Gnd. Headset mic ground (XLR5 pin 1) lands on Ch8 Hot and mic hot (XLR5 p…
- **Fix:** retitled as single-channel; dead-ended pins 6–25 individually; strengthened Tascam mis-mate and phantom warning; stated genders and DMX/RS-232 mis-mates — *source:* TASCAM DB-25 Pinout Standard PDF; RTS BP-325 manual p.13; Yamaha phantom

**#20 XLR5 to XLR3 — Beltpack Breakout (Mic In + Ear Out)** — C:ok D:minor F:ok P:minor R:ok
- [C] The two 'separate grounds' are not kept separate once both XLR3s plug into one mixer: XLR5 pin 1 (via mic-in XLR3 pin 1/3) and XLR5 pin 3 (via ear-out XLR3 pin 1/3) are bonded through the mixer's chassis. On an RTS BP-325 both are 'Common', so this is only a p…
- [C] Level: the pack headphone output is up to 8 V p-p (~+8 dBu). The note says 'pad or trim at the mixer' but does not say to use a LINE input — into a mic input this clips.
- [D] Missing caution on the Ear OUT channel: at least one Clear-Com beltpack family puts DC on the headphone output pin and uses the mic-ground pin as DC return. The ear-out tap would deliver that DC to the mixer's XLR3 pin 2 relative to pin 1/3. The build is verif…
- [F] Informational: unusual build; no manufacturer sells a pack-side breakout (Point Source Audio's ADP-5FX3M-TRS and ADP-4MX3M-TRS Y-cables are the headset-side equivalent, mic to XLR3 plus TRS for the ears). Pin usage (1/2 mic, 3/4 left ear) matches the RTS BP-32…
- [P] The Ear OUT leg terminates in a MALE XLR3 that plugs straight into a mixer mic input. If that channel's 48 V phantom is on, 48 V via 6.81 kΩ (~7 mA) is forced into the beltpack's headphone-amplifier output on XLR5 pin 4, and another ~7 mA into the earphone ret…
- [P] The Mic IN leg is a female XLR3, so (as in builds 3 and 9) a party-line cable's male end fits it: 30 VDC lands on pack pin 2 (mic preamp) and the pin 1→XLR3 pin 3 jumper shorts the PL audio pin to ground. Notes do not mention it.
- [R] No phantom caution on the ear-out leg: the male XLR3 fits a mic input, and with 48 V on, pin 2 (via 6.8 kohm) sits on the pack's headphone amplifier output and pin 3 (grounded in this cable) sinks phantom current into the pack common.
- [R] Notes say 'two separate grounds — keep them apart', but when both XLR3s land on the same mixer their pin 1s are bonded by the mixer chassis, so the cable cannot keep pack pin 1 and pin 3 apart; on a BP-325 both are commons anyway.
- [R] The male XLR5 end is drawn as a female face; the mirrored insert risks a 1<->5 / 2<->4 swap at the pack end, putting the right headphone output on the mic-in leg.
- **Fix:** added phantom, level, party-line and RS-700 DC cautions — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13; RS-701 QRG; Yamaha phantom; Clear-Com SF 158784598

- [D] The xlr5 connector is defined faceGender 'female' (the pack's socket), but the build note says the cable end is a male XLR5. The face view the builder sees is therefore the mirror of the plug they are soldering.
- [F] Pin assignment matches field practice for an RTS/Clear-Com 5-pin stereo pack to consumer stereo phones (pin 4 → Tip, pin 5 → Ring, pin 3 → Sleeve, pins 1/2 open), and the 'never bridge 4 and 5' caution is correct. Missing caution: consumer 16–32 Ω earbuds are …

- [R] Worst realistic failure: consumer 16–32 ohm earbuds hang on a pack headphone amp specified for a 50–600 ohm load at 8 V p-p. Below-minimum load plus in-ear transducers: amp clips/heats and a call signal or open mic lands at ear-damaging SPL. Wiring is correct;…
- [R] 3.5 mm end gender is not stated. Headphones carry a male plug, so this end must be a female (inline) jack; a tech following the XLR gender note pattern may build a male–male lead that cannot take headphones and, worse, invites plugging the 3.5 mm male into a p…
- **Fix:** added impedance/level caution and 3.5 mm gender — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13

**#29 TRS to XLR4 — Monitor Feed to Headset Earphone** — C:ok D:minor F:minor P:minor R:dangerous
- [C] Circuit is right (Tip→pin 4, Sleeve→pin 3, Ring floating, pin 1 and 2 open). Missing load caution: the earphone is a 200–400 Ω passive load, so the TRS source must be a headphone amplifier, not a line output; a line output into 200 Ω will sag and distort.

- [R] Gender and mirror trap: the headset side is a FEMALE XLR4, so this cable needs a MALE XLR4, but the connector drawing is the female face and the build does not state gender. A tech who wires a male insert by reading the female face mirrored swaps pins 1↔4 and …
- **Fix:** added level/hearing caution and gender/mirror statement — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13 (8 V p-p into 50–600 Ω); Clear-Com SF 158841161

**#37 RJ45 to BNC — NOT a Real SDI-over-Cat5 Path** — C:ok D:ok F:minor P:ok R:ok
- [C] Circuit reasoning (75 Ω single-ended vs 100 Ω balanced, no CMRR when a single-ended signal is strapped to one leg) is sound and the not_possible status is right. The note's '1.5–12 GHz' is loose: SDI bit rates are 270 Mb/s (SD) to 11.88 Gb/s (12G); the figures…
- [D] Notes say SDI 'runs at 1.5–12 GHz'. The SDI standards define bit rates of 1.485–11.88 Gbit/s; the fundamental frequency is half the bit rate (~0.74–5.94 GHz). The unit is wrong.
- [D] Cited source 'Belden — Key to Video Cable Performance' could not be fetched (403), so it is unverified; the SMPTE ST 2110 page does not mention ST 2022-6.
- [F] Core verdict (a wire-only BNC↔RJ45 bodge is not an SDI path) matches field practice; nobody sells one. But the note 'For real SDI over Cat5, use an ACTIVE extender' overstates: passive HD-SDI baluns are a stock rental/installer item, and passive composite balu…
- [F] Note says SDI 'runs at 1.5–12 GHz'. SDI rates are 1.5–12 Gb/s (fundamental ≈ half the bit rate); engineers will read the unit as wrong.
- **Fix:** unit fix — *source:* SMPTE rates
- **Fix:** softened the 'active extender only' claim — *source:* field practice (agents); Canare SDI table

- [C] Build omits two assembly facts from the cited instruction that bear on safety of the earth path: the PE conductor is stripped/cut longer than L and N so it makes first and breaks last, and the connector is specified for 2.5 mm² (AWG 12) conductors — the build …
- [D] Label/gender wording: the 'use' text calls the NAC3FCA and NAC3FCB 'male' cable connectors. Neutrik designates both cable connectors as female (F = female cable; the 'MP' chassis parts are male). The electrical content (L→L, N→N, PE→PE; blue A-type = POWER IN,…

- [D] Connector face check: powercon facePositions L top-left (35,35), N top-right (65,35), PE bottom (50,68) are consistent with the mating face of the NAC3FCA/FCB cable connector (mirror of Neutrik's rear WIRING view N-left / PE-bottom-centre / L-right). No disagr…
- [F] The 'use' text calls both cable ends 'male' ('male BLUE NAC3FCA', 'male GREY NAC3FCB'). Neutrik classifies both NAC3FCA and NAC3FCB cable connectors as female; the chassis parts NAC3MPA/NAC3MPB are the male side. Not a wiring error, but a rental-house tech rea…
- [F] Both cited parts are discontinued; the current field parts are NAC3FXXA-W-S/-L (blue, power-in) and NAC3FXXB-W-S/-L (grey, power-out), same pinout and keying. Also BDA 452 specifies the PE conductor stripped 3 mm longer than L/N (first-make/last-break earth), …
- [P] The safety sentence names the harmless mis-build and omits the dangerous one. 'Never IN-to-IN' describes a blue–blue (NAC3FCA both ends) cable, which can only mate two power-IN chassis (two loads, no source) — dead and useless but not hazardous. The hazardous …
- [P] Wire-colour citation is to a withdrawn standard.
- [P] Both cited connectors are discontinued; the TRUE1 hot-plug claim is correct and could cite Neutrik directly.
- [R] Pin assignment and face orientation check out: BDA 452's rear wiring drawing reads N, earth, L left-to-right, which mirrors to L-left / N-right on the mating face as the app draws it, and the insert carries moulded L / N / earth marks. Worst realistic failure …
- [R] Title and 'use' say 'Extension'. A standard powerCON cable connector mates only with a chassis connector of its own type; FCA will not mate FCB and there is no standard-powerCON inline coupler, so this cable cannot extend another cable. A tech who reads 'exten…
- [R] Hot-plugging caution in the notes is correct and matches Neutrik; keep it. Minor wording: the notes cite 'IEC 60446' for wire colours; that standard was withdrawn and merged into IEC 60445 (brown L, blue N, green-yellow PE unchanged). No safety effect.
- **Fix:** 'Never IN-to-IN' named the harmless mis-build; grey–grey (OUT-to-OUT) is the dangerous one; cable connectors are female per Neutrik — *source:* Neutrik NAC3FCA / NAC3FCB product pages
- **Fix:** standard citation updated; PE-first-make, current part numbers, blue-only mains lead, jumper-not-extension — *source:* Neutrik NAC3FCB page; BDA 452 (via agents)

**#79 Serial Data Grey — RS-232/422** — C:major D:major F:minor P:major R:major
- [C] As an RS-232 adapter it cannot work: DE-9 pin 5 (Signal Ground) is N/C and nothing else carries ground. RS-232 is single-ended and every signal is measured against that ground. The title and 'use' claim RS-232 or RS-422, so half the stated job fails.
- [C] For RS-422 the wiring is correct and pair-clean against the Kalypso serial ports: DE-9 3 (TX+) and 8 (TX−) share RJ45 green pair 3/6, DE-9 7 (RX+) and 2 (RX−) share blue pair 5/4. Only the common (DE-9 6) is not carried. Note the descriptions carry no signal n…
- [D] The RS-232 half of the title is not supported: DE-9 pin 5 (RS-232 signal ground) is N/C and no other wire carries ground, so an RS-232 link through this adapter has no return path and will not work. Pins 7 and 8 carried are RTS/CTS, not ground.
- [D] No signal reference is carried for RS-422 either (Sony 9-pin pins 4/6 and frame ground 1/9 all N/C); add a caution that this works only where both ends share a ground.
- [D] Pin names displayed (RxD/TxD/RTS/CTS) are RS-232 DTE names; for the RS-422 use the pins are Rx−/Tx+/Rx+/Tx−, and the wiring descs name no signal at all.
- [F] No reachable source for either end. The Kalypso HD/Duo manual documents its Serial 1–8 ports as D-9 female RS-422, not RJ-45, so an RJ-45 'at the Kalypso end' only makes sense for a Kalypso Classic frame or an intermediate patch field — neither is documented i…
- [F] The RS-232 half of the title is not supported by the wiring: there is no signal-ground conductor (DE-9 pin 5 N/C) and DE-9 7/8 (RTS/CTS in RS-232) are carried instead. Every published RS-232-over-RJ45 convention dedicates RJ45 pins to ground.
- [P] Stated use 'RS-232 or RS-422' cannot both be true with these four wires. For RS-232 the cable has no signal ground: DE-9 pin 5 is N/C and nothing carries it to the RJ45. RS-232 is single-ended, referenced to pin 5; without it the ±12 V data return runs through…
- [P] This shell is physically identical to 80, 81 and 82 (all male DE-9 → RJ45, all for the TSI-1000/Kalypso job) and only this one records a shell colour. If an RS-232 shell (81/82) is used at the other end of the Cat5 from this RS-422 shell, RS-232 levels (±5–15 …
- [P] PoE/Ethernet misplug is safe for this shell: mode A needs pair 1/2 (open) and mode B needs pair 7/8 (open), so no PSE will power up. Note only.
- [R] Stated job includes RS-232, but DE-9 pin 5 (Signal Ground) is N/C and nothing else carries ground: the Cable Wholesale shell is an unshielded plastic adapter with eight flying leads and no shield path to the RJ45, so a UTP Cat5 run gives the two ends no common…
- [R] Diagram misbuild risk: the DE-9 face names pins 7/8 'RTS/CTS' and 2/3 'RxD/TxD', which is wrong for the RS-422 use (there 2/7 and 3/8 are the differential pairs). A tech trying to 'fix' a dead RS-422 link from those labels will swap the wrong wires.
- **Fix:** retitled RS-422 only (RS-232 impossible without ground); signal names in descs; deadEnds per pin; shell-mixing caution — *source:* Kalypso manual p.68 (Serial RS-422 pinout); Lammert Bies; TI SNLA039

**#80 RS-422 Null Crossover — TSI to Kalypso** — C:major D:major F:major P:major R:major
- [C] Swapping only DE-9 pins 2 and 3 is not an RS-422 null. On the Kalypso serial port pin 2 is RX− and pin 3 is TX+: they are opposite-polarity legs of two different pairs, so the swap neither exchanges TX with RX nor keeps polarity. Used against the straight grey…
- [C] The crossover as drawn splits both differential pairs across two cable pairs: RJ45 green pair 3/6 carries DE-9 2 (RX−) and 8 (TX−); blue pair 4/5 carries DE-9 3 (TX+) and 7 (RX+). Each RS-422 pair is therefore untwisted against its own return and twisted with …
- [C] Note text 'Tx/Rx pair is swapped ... Pins 2↔3 on the RJ45 side' describes an RS-232 TxD/RxD swap, not an RS-422 pair swap, and misleads the builder.
- [D] Swapping only DE-9 pins 2 and 3 is not an RS-422 null: pins 2 and 3 are opposite-polarity wires of two different pairs (2 = Rx−/A, 3 = Tx+/B). Mated through a straight grey adapter (build 79) this gives: near pin 8 (Tx−) ↔ far pin 8 (Tx−) — two drivers tied to…
- [D] Notes say 'Pins 2↔3 on the RJ45 side', but the change is DE-9 pins 2 and 3 moving between RJ45 pins 3 and 4; the note misdescribes the build.
- [D] Tying two RS-422 drivers together (pin 8 ↔ pin 8 via the far grey adapter) puts both outputs in contention; most RS-422 drivers are short-circuit limited so damage is unlikely, but it should be called out as a stress on the Kalypso and TSI ports.
- [F] Swapping only DE-9 pins 2 and 3 cannot make an RS-422 null/crossover. RS-422 is two differential pairs; a crossover must exchange the whole TX pair with the whole RX pair (four conductors). Moving two single conductors either flips polarity within one pair or,…
- [F] Note text is wrong about which pins move: it says 'Pins 2↔3 on the RJ45 side' but the RJ45 pins that change relative to the grey adapter are 3 and 4 (DE-9 2 goes to RJ45 3 instead of 4, DE-9 3 to RJ45 4 instead of 3).
- [P] Crossing only DE-9 2↔3 is an RS-232-style null, not an RS-422 one. On a Sony/SMPTE 207M port the pairs are 2/7 (Receive A/B) and 3/8 (Transmit B/A). With this shell at the Kalypso end and a grey 79 at the TSI end on straight Cat5: Kalypso 2 (Rx A) ↔ TSI 3 (Tx …
- [P] Notes say 'Pins 2↔3 on the RJ45 side' — the RJ45 pins swapped are 3 and 4 (DE-9 2 goes to RJ45 3, DE-9 3 to RJ45 4). RJ45 pin 2 is unused.
- [P] No shell colour or label recorded; identical in appearance to 79, 81, 82. See 79/81 for the RS-232-into-RS-422 receiver damage path (RS-232 ±15 V on receivers rated −7..+7 V common mode). PoE misplug is safe (1/2 and 7/8 open).
- [R] A 4-wire RS-422 crossover must swap the two differential PAIRS (Grass Valley: Rx pair 2/7 with Tx pair 3/8, i.e. 2↔8 and 3↔7). This build swaps only DE-9 2↔3 and leaves 7 and 8 straight, which splits each pair: Rx-(2) and Tx+(3) swap, Rx+(7) and Tx-(8) stay. A…
- [R] Three different adapters (80, 81, 82) all claim 'TSI-1000 to Kalypso at the Kalypso end' and none of the three names a shell colour (only 79 is 'grey'). A tech has a one-in-three pick between physically identical black-shell adapters, two of which cannot work …
- **Fix:** flagged UNVERIFIED: 2↔3-only swap is not an RS-422 null (all five angles agree); corrected the wrong note; proposed the whole-pair crossover; deadEnds per pin. Wiring left as recorded pending Jadon's answer (physical shell?) — *source:* Kalypso manual p.68; Ross GVG serial tally crossover table (agents)

- [F] The TxD/RxD cross itself is a correct RS-232 null modem relative to build 81 (DE-9 2 and 3 exchanged on RJ45 4/5), but like build 81 it has no Signal Ground conductor (DE-9 pin 5 N/C) and spends the third wire on DCD. A 3-wire null modem is defined as 2↔3, 3↔2…
- [F] Same Kalypso-end doubt as build 81: the Kalypso HD/Duo frame's only RS-232 port is the D-9 Diagnostic port (DCE); serial ports 1–8 are RS-422.
- [P] No signal ground conductor (DE-9 pin 5 N/C, no RJ45 pin carries ground); same fault and same consequence as build 81. The crossover itself (2→5, 3→4 against 81's 2→4, 3→5) is correct for a 3-wire null, but the standard 3-wire null is 2↔3, 3↔2 AND 5↔5.
- [P] RS-232 levels into RS-422 receivers if mixed with 79/80 — identical shells, no colour recorded. TxD (DE-9 3 → RJ45 4) on a far grey 79 lands on DE-9 2 (Rx A), and RJ45 5 on DE-9 7 (Rx B): ±12 V onto a receiver specified for −7..+7 V common mode. Notes do not w…
- [P] DCD (DE-9 1 → RJ45 8) is wired straight, so DCD input meets DCD input end-to-end: it does nothing on a DTE-to-DTE null. Harmless (inputs only), and with StudioHub+ +15 V on pin 8 it is a receiver input rated ±25 V. PoE is safe (signature too low, as in 81). No…
- [R] Same missing signal ground as build 81: DE-9 pin 5 N/C, no shield path in the Cable Wholesale shell, so this 3-wire 'null modem' is actually a 2-wire one. Lammert Bies' minimum null modem is 2↔3, 3↔2 AND 5↔5. Worst realistic failure: dead link, or a floating-r…
- [R] Two RS-232 adapters for the same two devices (81 straight, 82 crossed) with no shell colour and no statement of which one actually works between the TSI-1000 serial port and the Kalypso port. Only one can be right for a given port pair; the other guarantees Tx…
- **Fix:** flagged UNVERIFIED: no RS-232 signal ground; pin-1 wire may be the ground on a Sony-style port; deadEnds per pin; Kalypso port-type note — *source:* Kalypso manual p.67–68; Lammert Bies; TI SNLA039

**#83 Cronus RJ12 to RJ45** — C:minor D:major F:major P:major R:major
- [C] Pair integrity and polarity are consistent if the Cronus RJ-12 port follows the RTS RJ-12 convention: RJ12 1/6 (Data−/Data+) → RJ45 green pair 3/6, RJ12 2/5 (audio from matrix +/−) → RJ45 blue pair 5/4, RJ12 3/4 (audio to matrix +/−) → RJ45 orange pair 1/2. I …
- [C] This adapter's RJ45 assignment is incompatible with the rig's Adam KP shells (77/78) on a straight Cat5. 83 puts data on RJ45 3/6, from-matrix audio on 4/5 and to-matrix audio on 1/2; 77 expects data on 1/2, from-matrix on 3/6 and to-matrix on 4/5. Patched tog…
- [C] Pin numbering assumes a straight-through 6P6C cord between the Cronus jack and this adapter; a reversed (flat telephone-style) cord would flip every pair's polarity (1↔6, 2↔5, 3↔4), inverting the data pair.
- [D] The RJ45 assignment is incompatible with the rig's own Black/Yellow DE-9 shells (builds 77/78): run over Cat5 to a Black shell, Cronus Audio In (to-matrix, RJ12 3/4 → RJ45 1/2) lands on the keypanel's Data+/Data− (DE-9 1/2); Cronus Control/data (RJ12 1/6 → RJ4…
- [D] The RJ45 assignment also matches none of RTS's own RJ-45 conventions, and the title/use text 'Cronus timing/tally adapter' misdescribes the signals: a Cronus RJ-12 port carries keypanel data plus two audio pairs, nothing to do with timing or tally.
- [D] No wire carries a signal name — every desc gives only cable colours — so a builder cannot check polarity (e.g. that RJ12 2 is Audio Out+ and RJ12 5 is Audio Out−).
- [F] The pair mapping does not match RTS's published RJ-12↔RJ-45 relationship and will not inter-work with any RTS RJ-45 port or with builds 77/78. RTS puts the RJ-12 centre pair (3/4, TO MATRIX) on RJ-45 4/5, pair 2/5 (FROM MATRIX) on RJ-45 3/6, and data (1/6) on …
- [F] Description 'Cronus timing/tally adapter' is wrong: Cronus is an RTS 32-port digital intercom matrix whose RJ-12 ports carry keypanel audio and RS-485 control data, not timing or tally.
- [P] The RJ45 pair map (talk on orange 1/2, data on green 3/6, listen on blue 5/4) is incompatible with the Adam shells 77/78 (data orange 1/2, listen green 3/6, talk blue 4/5), with the ASG drawing, and with RTS's XCP-48-RJ45. The 'use' says this feeds an 'RJ/9-pi…
- [P] No caution about an Ethernet/PoE misplug. Mode-A PoE (pairs 1/2 and 3/6) sees the Cronus audio-in pair and the RS-485 data pair, both connected; if their combined impedance falls in the 19–26.5 kΩ detection window, 48 V is applied between the matrix port's aud…
- [P] Intended-use voltages: none. The Cronus RJ-12 carries only RS-485 data and balanced audio; no DC is listed on any pin. Wiring is correct as a Cronus/XCP-955 RJ-12 pair-preserving extension (each RJ-12 pair stays on one RJ45 twisted pair). Note only.
- [R] The adapter keeps the Cronus pairs intact (RJ45 1/2 = Audio In to matrix, 3/6 = Control/data, 4/5 = Audio Out from matrix) but uses a fourth RJ45 convention that matches none of the rig's own 9-pin shells, RTS's XCP-48-RJ45, or the Clear-Com/ASG drawing. Worst…
- [R] Use text calls Cronus a 'timing/tally adapter'. Cronus is an RTS 32-port digital matrix intercom; the RJ-12 carries 4-wire intercom audio and RS-485 keypanel data. The label will send a tech looking for a tally system and hide the intercom-output-contention ri…
- [R] Missing caution: an off-the-shelf flat telephone RJ12 cord between the Cronus port and this adapter is usually reversed (pin 1↔6, 2↔5, 3↔4). RTS keypanel/matrix cabling must be straight pin-1-to-pin-1; a reversed cord inverts the polarity of all three pairs, s…
- **Fix:** use text corrected (Cronus is a matrix intercom, not timing/tally); signal names in descs; flagged UNVERIFIED and incompatible with shells 77/78; RTS RJ-12→RJ-45 relationship and a compatible rewire recorded. Wiring left as recorded pending Jadon's answer — *source:* RTS KP-5032 manual p.19 (RJ-45 and RJ-12 columns); RTS XCP-48-RJ45 manual p.3
## Round 2 (76 builds: every build that changed or drew a disagreement in round 1; fresh agents, current data)

Result: 25 builds cleared by all five angles; 51 drew disagreements — 1 'dangerous' (#10: no phantom caution on the new LTC build), 21 'major' (the four unverified shell adapters, #32's Yost/Cisco contradiction, and two red-team face-mirror claims that were themselves wrong — see below), the rest minor wording. No wiring fault was found in the round-1 changes; one wiring change came out of it (#50 Ring tied to Sleeve). Two red-team agents disagreed with each other about the face drawings: one said the female XLR3 face has pins 1 and 2 swapped (it does not — it matches the StudioHub+ guide's female drawing and Jadon's physical check), the other correctly pointed out that a female mating face is what a MALE insert looks like from the solder side, so the round-1 'male insert is mirrored' notes were wrong and were rewritten.

- [P] Missing caution on powered RJ45 jacks, as for build 79: a HelixNet powerline port puts +30 V on RJ45 3/6 and −30 V on 4/5; here that is 60 V between Kalypso RX− (pin 2)/TX− (pin 8) and TX+ (pin 3)/RX+ (pin 7).
- [R] As wired this is not a crossover and cannot pass data; the build is still status 'standard' with a title promising a working null. Trace through a grey (79) shell at the far end: near DE-9 2 (RX−) → RJ45 3 → far DE-9 3 (TX+); near 3 (TX+) → RJ45 4 → far 2 (RX−…
- **Fix:** added powered-jack caution — *source:* Clear-Com HelixNet tip; PoE Wikipedia
- **Fix:** status standard → field_hack (all five angles: as recorded this is not an RS-422 null); wiring left as recorded pending Jadon — *source:* Kalypso manual p.68

**#81 RS-232 Adapter — TSI to Kalypso** — C:major D:major F:major P:minor R:major
- [C] RS-232 is single-ended and every signal is referenced to DE-9 pin 5; this shell carries no pin 5 and no shield, so the only candidate return is the DE-9 pin 1 wire. On the Kalypso's only RS-232 port (the Diagnostic port) pin 1 is DCD, a driver output, not grou…
- [C] TxD and RxD share one twisted pair (blue 4/5) while the return is on the brown pair — the two single-ended signals are coupled to each other instead of to their return. Workable for short, slow runs but it invites crosstalk; notes mention it.
- [D] No RS-232 signal ground is carried (DE-9 pin 5 open), so a single-ended RS-232 link cannot work; the only RS-232 port on the Kalypso frame is the Diagnostic port, where pin 1 is DCD, not chassis ground, so the notes' hypothesis that the pin-1 wire doubles as g…
- [F] No RS-232 signal ground is carried (DE-9 pin 5 N/C) and the only candidate ground wire (DE-9 pin 1 → RJ45 8) is DCD on the Kalypso Diagnostic port, so the link relies entirely on chassis grounding; status 'standard' contradicts the UNVERIFIED notes.
- [F] Pair assignment is this rig's own and is not compatible with the common RS-232-over-RJ45 adapters (Yost / Cisco console), which the notes do not mention: Yost puts signal ground on RJ45 4 and 5 and data on 3 and 6, this shell puts RxD/TxD on 4 and 5.
- [P] Missing caution: this shell uses RJ45 4, 5 and 8, which is exactly PoE Mode B's two pairs (4/5 and 7/8). A passive PoE injector or a mis-patch puts 44–57 V between RJ45 4/5 (DE-9 2 RxD / 3 TxD) and RJ45 8 (DE-9 pin 1 — chassis ground on a Sony-style port). Tha…
- [P] Missing caution on the DE-9 side: the male shell fits all nine female D-9s on a Kalypso frame, but only the Diagnostic port is RS-232. Plugged into Serial 1–8, the TSI's RS-232 TxD (±12 V, DE-9 3 via RJ45 5) drives the Kalypso's TX+ driver output (pin 3) and R…
- [R] The cable cannot work as an RS-232 link as recorded: RS-232 is single-ended against DE-9 pin 5, which is a dead end here, and the plastic shell/UTP has no other ground path. The only ground candidate is the pin-1 wire, which on the Kalypso's only RS-232 port (…
- [R] Red-team worst case: the title 'TSI to Kalypso at the Kalypso end' points the builder at the Kalypso's tally port, and the Kalypso manual's default tally port is Serial 5 — an RS-422 port, not RS-232. Plugging this shell there puts the TSI's RS-232 TxD (±5–12 …
- **Fix:** pin-1 ground hypothesis fails on the Diagnostic port; port-type, Yost and powered-jack cautions; status → field_hack — *source:* Kalypso manual p.67–68; Yost page; PoE Wikipedia

**#82 RS-232 Null Crossover — TSI to Kalypso** — C:major D:major F:major P:minor R:major
- [C] The 2/3 cross is a correct RS-232 TxD/RxD null relative to shell 81 (near DE-9 2 → RJ45 5 → far DE-9 3; near 3 → RJ45 4 → far 2), but like shell 81 it carries no DE-9 pin 5 signal ground and no shield. A 3-wire null modem is 2↔3, 3↔2, 5↔5; here the third wire …

- [F] Not compatible with Yost/Cisco RS-232-over-RJ45 adapters (data on RJ45 4/5 where those carry signal ground); not mentioned in notes.
- [P] Same missing RJ45-side caution as build 81: the shell occupies RJ45 4, 5 and 8 — PoE Mode B's pairs. 44–57 V from a passive injector lands between DE-9 2/3 (RxD/TxD) and DE-9 1 (chassis ground on a Sony-style port), beyond RS-232's ±25 V withstand. HelixNet 30…

- [R] Red-team worst case, as 81: title directs it to the Kalypso, whose tally port (default Serial 5) is RS-422. Here the cross makes it worse — TSI TxD arrives on RJ45 4 → DE-9 2 = Kalypso RX− (receiver input, RX+ left floating, ±12 V single-ended on one leg), and…
- **Fix:** pin-1 ground hypothesis fails on the Diagnostic port; port-type, Yost and powered-jack cautions; status → field_hack — *source:* Kalypso manual p.67–68; Yost page; PoE Wikipedia

## Round 3 (70 power-carrying builds; fresh power-and-damage and red-team agents)

Result: 65 of 70 agreed with the current data; 5 drew disagreements (#3, #9 — the 'never into the LINE jack' caution described an impossible female-to-female mating and the mixer-output mis-plug was missing; #4 — the party-line and level cautions that build 8 carried were missing; #29 — the RTS female headset jack, 12 V power outputs and speaker outputs the male XLR4 / TRS plug also fit; #49 — no notes at all). Most 'agree' verdicts came with minor caution additions, all applied. Every power-carrying build then went to round 4 if any angle had disagreed in round 2 or 3.

**#1 XLR3 Mic Cable (Straight-Through)** — P:ok R:ok
- [P] Notes understate the worst intercom case: HelixNet XLR3 ports put +30 V on pin 2 AND −30 V on pin 3, i.e. 60 V across a mic coil or a balanced input (pins 2–3), not '15–30 V DC on pin 2'. A 200 Ω dynamic mic across it draws ~300 mA.
- **Fix:** replaced a 'meter it' instruction with the documented source or a manual check (Jadon: sources, not meters) — *source:* RS-701 QRG; RTS BP-325 manual p.13; Sony HXCU-100 p.8; Kalypso manual p.67–68
- **Fix:** HelixNet 60 V case — *source:* Clear-Com HelixNet tip

**#2 XLR3 to XLR5 - Pass-Through** — P:ok R:ok
- [P] Same HelixNet gap as build 1: a HelixNet port on the XLR3 end energises XLR5 pins 2/3 with +30 V and −30 V (60 V differential), not just '30 V DC on pin 2'. A 5-pin headset mic (pin 2 to pin 1) sees 30 V; a DMX receiver across pins 2/3 sees 60 V.
- **Fix:** HelixNet 60 V case — *source:* Clear-Com HelixNet tip

**#3 XLR3 to XLR5 - Mic into Intercom Beltpack (Mic Input)** — P:ok R:minor
- [P] Missing mis-plug: the female XLR3 also fits a mixer/aux OUTPUT (male chassis), the obvious thing to try to get program into the pack. The pin 3→pin 1 jumper then shorts the output's cold leg to ground, which Rane says can damage an active balanced output stage…
- [R] Realistic damage path missing: the FEMALE XLR3 end also accepts a mixer/playback OUTPUT (male XLR). People do feed program into a beltpack mic input this way; the XLR3 pin 3 -> XLR5 pin 1 jumper then shorts the output's cold leg to ground.
- [R] Party-line warning describes an impossible action: 'Never plug this into the pack's 3-pin intercom LINE jack' - the cable's XLR3 is female and the LINE jack is female (BP-325 XLR-3-31). The real path is a LINE cable's male end plugged into this adapter's femal…
- **Fix:** the old caution described an impossible mating (female into female); added the mixer-output mis-plug — *source:* Clear-Com HelixNet tip; Rane 151; RTS BP-325 manual p.13

**#4 XLR3 to XLR5 - Balanced Feed to Both Ears of a Headset** — P:major R:major
- [P] The build's cable end that fits an intercom LINE connector puts 30 V DC across both earpieces with a direct ground return. CC-400 200 Ω earpieces in parallel = 100 Ω → 300 mA, 9 W; PH-88 300 Ω → 150 Ω → 200 mA, 6 W; with the build's own '75 Ω' figure, 400 mA /…
- [R] Worst realistic failure is missing from the notes: the female XLR3 end accepts a party-line cable's male end. 30 V DC on XLR3 pin 2 then goes to XLR5 pins 4 AND 5 with pin 1 (common) on XLR5 pin 3, i.e. 30 V straight across BOTH earphone coils - 75-150 mA per …
- [R] Hearing/level hazard present on build 8 but absent here: a +4 dBu (or clipping +20 dBu) line output drives 200-400 ohm earphones with no volume control in the path.
- **Fix:** added the party-line and level cautions build 8 already carried; gender stated — *source:* RTS BP-325 manual p.13; Clear-Com SF 158784598; Clear-Com CC-300 page

**#5 XLR3 to 1/4" TRS — Balanced Audio** — P:ok R:ok
- [R] Worst realistic failure: the TRS end goes into a mono TS jack or a console INSERT jack. The TS case (Ring = XLR pin 3 grounded, shorting a non-floating active output) is already in the notes. The insert case is not: a TRS plug in an insert jack puts the XLR ou…

- **Fix:** insert-jack caution; gender/face note — *source:* Rane 109; connector data

## Round 4 (55 builds: every build that drew any disagreement in rounds 2 or 3; fresh agents, all five angles)

Run by the Fable chat before its usage limit; applied and stamped by the Opus close-out chat (2026-11-09). 47 of 50 agents returned: the documentation, field-practice and red-team agents for the speaker/power cluster (#54 #55 #56 #76) hit the Fable usage limit, so those four builds have only power and circuit verdicts and cannot be verified.

Result: 20 of 55 builds cleared by all five angles (#3 #9 #17 #20 #21 #24 #25 #27 #29 #31 #32 #33 #39 #41 #42 #49 #50 #52 #53 #70). 31 drew a disagreement (13 major, 45 minor angle-verdicts, none dangerous); 4 were incomplete. Every fix below cites a source a round 4 reviewer opened. Builds round 4 disagreed with stay UNVERIFIED until a fresh five-angle review; agreeing reviewers' minor suggestions were applied to #9 #20 #21 #24 #25 #49, which stay verified.

Found while applying round 4 (not raised by any reviewer): #31 and #32 told the builder to solder a FEMALE D-sub from the app's SOLDER SIDE view. The app draws D-subs as the MALE face and its SOLDER SIDE view is the mirror of that, i.e. a male shell's cups; a female shell's cups match the FACE VIEW (DSubConnector.jsx; the toggle's own caption says the same). Corrected; #31/#32 are UNVERIFIED until re-reviewed.

- [R] Worst realistic failure: a 5-pin headset or DMX fixture plugged into the energised 5-pin end of a party-line / HelixNet / phantom run (30 V or ±30 V across a 200 Ω headset mic). The notes already warn about exactly this, so it is not a disagreement. What is mi…
